including real dropdown clicks, Tab focus, alternate wiring, partial failure,
stale success, malformed sources, reset/resume and exact retention. Development
native R7 passed 89 actions and 24 captures. Actual reviewed screens include
100/150% dark and narrow 200% Light layouts. Wide topology lines reflect cable
ends. Narrow cards name current neighbors, and full request/reply traces name
the destination. Final all-target Clippy passed. Python's first 1,057-check run
found one stale generated teaching inventory. After regeneration, all 1,057
checks and strict coverage/inventory checks passed. A separate Fedora392
candidate and its installed checks are next.

Current certification parents are 54 planned, 81 partial and one supporting
explanation. No full objective is complete. The skill map has 351 partial,
12 supporting and 877 open references. These references overlap and do not
count missing labs. CCNA 1.1 gains partial router, Layer-2 switch and endpoint
practice; a server's role has supporting explanation only. Layer-3 switching,
firewalls/IPS, wireless, controllers, PoE and other roadmap gaps remain open.

Next coherent work is the existing CCNA 6.7 structured-inventory gap. Establish
bounded JSON-to-simulated-switch capability before authoring its assignments.
Private groundwork has no catalog or objective credit. Other operating systems
remain deferred. Separate-process cable reopening, physical-device accessibility,
screen readers and independent novice-comprehension acceptance remain unverified.
[Teaching, sources and evidence](DEVICE_PATH_LESSONS.md).

## Local-mail unit verified in development — 2026-11-10

The source has 390 labs across 149 units. CCNA has 89 labs, LPIC-1 has 131,
LPIC-2 has 93 and Bash/Shell has 77. Three new assignments teach team aliases,
per-user forwarding with a local copy, and recovery from a stale lookup and
alias loop while preserving protected mail. They use a bounded in-process
recipient graph, saved virtual files and a separate alias lookup. All 387
preceding scenario objects and 148 unit values remain exact.

Five model tests, seven content/persistence tests and a UI-guidance regression
passed. The final native walkthrough completed 110 actions and 28 captures.
It covers all three learning screens, independent drafts, partial delivery,
retry, Challenge, keyboard editing, save/readback and dark100/150 plus narrow
Light200 layouts. Final all-target Clippy, 1,053 Python checks, strict coverage
and the teaching inventory passed. The Fedora390 package passed private upgrade/reinstall, installed mail and
journal workflows and separate-process NFS reopening. [Lessons and evidence](LOCAL_MAIL_LESSONS.md).

LPIC-1 108.3 has partial local alias, forwarding and newaliases practice.
MTA roles and program names have supporting explanation. Remote delivery,
mail clients, submission syntax, queue inspection and wider MTA behavior remain
open. The current certification map has 55 planned parents, 80 partial parents
and one supporting explanation. Its 347 partial, 11 supporting and 882 open
references overlap and do not count missing labs. No objective is marked
complete. Other operating systems remain deferred, with screen-reader,
physical-device and independent novice acceptance unverified.

## Copied-journal recovery verified in development — 2026-11-10

The source has 387 labs across 148 units. CCNA has 89 labs, LPIC-1 has 128,
LPIC-2 has 93 and Bash/Shell has 77. The new journal exercise locates a boot
from its time window, filters service errors, diagnoses wrong queries and
preserves separate practice and assignment drafts. Three model and five
authored/persistence tests, retained Chrony and catalog checks, and 21 classic
logging tests passed. Final native verification completed 45 actions and ten
captures, including dark100/150 and narrow Light200. Final all-target Clippy,
1,049 Python tests, coverage validation and the per-lab inventory passed.
The Fedora387 package passed private upgrade/reinstall, installed journal and
Chrony workflows, and separate-process NFS reopening.
[Lesson and evidence](COPIED_JOURNAL_LESSON.md).

The map adds partial copied-journal recovery under LPIC-1 108.2 K7 and extends
its existing query/service/priority references. It also records the four
existing classic rsyslog lessons under K1 and K2, using retained tests.
There are 56 planned certification parents, 79 partial and one supporting
explanation. The granular map has 342 partial, seven supporting and 891 open
references. These references overlap and do not determine a remaining lab
count. No certification objective is marked complete.

## Linux time-source diagnosis — 2026-11-10

The source has 386 labs across 147 units. The new LPIC-1 time-source unit adds
one assignment for a reachable but unselected chrony server. Learners inspect
source reach and selection, repair the saved list, apply it, and verify the
approved primary and retained backup. Core, persistence and native development
checks passed. The Fedora386 package passed its installed workflows, private
upgrade and reinstall, and separate-process NFS reopening. [Scope and evidence](CHRONY_SOURCE_LESSON.md).

## Rescue startup repair in the source catalog — 2026-11-10

The source contains 385 labs across 146 units. CCNA has 89 labs, LPIC-1 has 126,
LPIC-2 has 93, and Bash/Shell has 77. The new LPIC-2 exercise repairs a failed
ordinary startup from the supplied rescue target and verifies both outcomes
without changing the saved default. It preserves all 384 preceding scenarios
and 145 unit values. Four authored and 61 retained manager tests passed.
The final native run completed 84 actions and nine captures. It exercised
keyboard editing, separate practice, a wrong but valid configuration,
invalidation, retry and save/readback. Final coverage is partial for LPIC-2
202.2 service initialization and rescue use. Emergency and mount recovery
remain open. [Lesson and evidence](RESCUE_STARTUP_LESSON.md).

The separate Fedora package passed private upgrade, reinstall, the installed
rescue and temporary GRUB flows, and separate-process NFS reopening. The next
independent work is LPIC-1 time-source diagnosis. See [the checkpoint](CHECKPOINT.md).

## Temporary GRUB recovery in the source catalog — 2026-11-10

The source has 384 labs and 145 units. CCNA has 89 labs, LPIC-1 has 126, LPIC-2
has 92, and Bash/Shell has 77. The learner edits a temporary Linux command,
checks the inspection kernel and root handoff, and verifies an ordinary boot
from the saved menu. The first native review found hidden command text and
repetitive failure messages. The revised editor wraps the command and leads
with the first blocked stage. Both development walkthroughs completed 34
actions and 10 captures. Six new core and persistence tests, six retained
menu tests and seven retained UI tests passed. All earlier 383 labs and 144
units compare exactly. [Lesson and evidence](GRUB_TEMPORARY_LESSON.md).

The current coverage map adds narrow LPIC-2 202.2.K10 practice for temporary
kernel arguments. It keeps GRUB shells and actual rescue or emergency service
behavior open. The separate Fedora candidate passed its private upgrade and
reinstall, installed GRUB and removable EFI workflows, and separate-process NFS
reopening. See [the checkpoint](CHECKPOINT.md) for launch and package identity.

## Independent removable EFI recovery — 383 labs, 144 units

The LPIC-2 path now has a practical removable recovery assignment after the
named EFI and service startup prerequisites. Learners diagnose two different
missing-disk dependencies, repair the default x64 loader, and verify the recovery
path and untouched normal startup. [Scope and Fedora evidence](EFI_REMOVABLE_LESSON.md).

The next recovery batch will model one-time GRUB menu edits and compare the
edited boot with a later ordinary boot from the saved menu. A kernel request for
a recovery target and the services actually reached are separate observations.
The existing simulator can verify the former. Rescue or emergency service
behavior requires a suitable runtime model before it receives coverage.
Alternate bootloaders and PXE remain a separate 202.3 gap.

# Roadmap continuation after the accepted209 catalog

## Links: implemented; visual correction pending in next candidate

Four LPIC-1 lessons in **Names, inodes and safe link handovers** follow the file
handover foundations. All three views have lesson-specific teaching, worked
practice commands, an optional virtual editor and independent state-based tasks.

| ID | Practical task |
| --- | --- |
| lpic1-links-identity | Shared live names versus independent archival copies |
| lpic1-links-relative | Diagnose a dangling relative symbolic target |
| lpic1-links-move | Move original identity and repair its stale symbolic pointer |
| lpic1-links-handover | Retire a release name, retain its inode/snapshot and switch a pointer |

The existing bounded inode VFS is reused. Command parsing is literal and explicit:
ln, ln -s, cp, mv, rm, stat, stat -L, readlink, cat, ls and pwd, with quoted
operands and --. Unsupported options, pipelines, expansions and shell syntax fail
honestly. Directory structure is fixed. No overwrite shorthand, permissions,
mount boundaries, open-descriptor lifetime or full GNU metadata is claimed.
No host commands, filesystem changes or network activity arise from learner input.

Shared/copy outcomes include multiple future-write probes on cloned simulated
state. Relative/absolute valid symbolic targets and equivalent inode-preserving
repairs pass. Copies masquerading as aliases, dangling pointers, lost original
identity and damaged retained files fail. Source-bound history is replayed on
load; unsaved editor changes invalidate current success. Practice worlds/drafts
are separate from assignments. Existing209 objects and56 units remain exact.
A nested JSON inode-key codec was corrected; existing link semantics are unchanged.

Current review library213/57: CCNA65, LPIC-1 48, LPIC-2 36, Bash64.
The201/54 normal baseline and209/56 accepted baseline are explicitly frozen for
historical verification. Neither is silently relabeled as the new review library.
The compiled candidate defaults to the actual current review catalog.
